<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I will be failing in my duty if I do not compliment the previous administration under the leadership of Mr G Krog and Mr A K Singh, for the efficient way they dealt with the problem in my area, especially with regard to the school building. They corrected the problem and I appreciate it. Furthermore, this may be my last speech on this budget. I will be seeking re-election, but goodness knows whether I will be back.</p>

<p>At the outset I want to say that I cannot defend the hon the Minister here this afternoon, after hearing the hon member for Lenasia East. [Interjections.] I do not say that it is rubbish.</p>

<p>It is an investigating report that has been published. If the hon the Minister is misleading this House, I feel he should resign. Parliament is not a place where hon members should be misled. I believe in clean administration. I will stand here and fight for clean administration. Clean administration must be seen, and not talked about.</p>

<p>If the director&#x2019;s daughter was involved in this matter, and if the evidence is true, then there should be a committee of enquiry or a select committee of Parliament to investigate this. If the director is guilty, the very same should be done to him as was done to other directors.</p>

<p>Hon members are going to seek re-election. I do not believe that the own affairs system will still be in existence in the year 2000. The own affairs education system must be abolished. We in this country need one President, one Minister of Education, one Minister of Health and one Minister of Police. We need one Ministry for everything. This is not my election manifesto. I am merely stating what I believe in.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>It is a good manifesto!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>This tricameral system has separated us from our people. I have said this previously. We have forgotten about our communities back home. I want to be honest. Hon members have become opportunistic. [Interjections.] I have said this in 1984 and 1985. It is on record in Hansard. Hon members are not interested in their communities anymore. They are interested in Mercedes-Benz cars. [Interjections.] They want to seek positions so that they can ask: &#x201C;What do I get?&#x201D;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_local_government_and_agriculture">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF LOCAL GOVERNMENT AND AGRICULTURE</person>:</from>

<p>Well, you have two schools in your area!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>This is not only about schools. The people of Isipingo are hard-working people&#x2014;not this House of Delegates. Mr Krog is in charge of this aspect. I have mentioned this. The schools have been built, yes. However, we only have one school in Malagassy that is being built now. It was planned as far back as 1979. It is because of the hard work of the people and the council of Isipingo that this has finally come about. We have hardly had any assistance from his administration.</p>

<p>Look at the people of Malagasy. Look at how my people are suffering. I am grateful that they are now building our school, and I respect them for that. However, look at where they are building our schools. They build our schools in areas where burglaries are commonplace. Thieves break into the schools. They build schools on river embankments.</p>

<p>When I came here in 1984, I never would have thought that Untag would be in Namibia today. However, within five years this became a reality. What is going to happen to the Republic of South Africa?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>The ANC will take over!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from><span class="col_8571-8572" refersTo="page_0311"/>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>There will be an ANC government in this country before the end of the century! This is definitely going to happen. Why do we not learn to live together and adapt? Why do we not open our schools? The hon the Minister said that certain criteria are applied. In my area we have made an application, but it seems that it is not what you know, but who you know that counts. In my area there were Black parents and unfortunately these parents were from the working class. They sent their children to a pre-primary school in my area. Automatically, one imagines that these children who left pre-primary school wanted to go to the primary school in my area. What happened? They were rejected! During that very same week, I made representations to the Black community for those children to be accepted. When are we going to start living together?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#govender">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M GOVENDER</person>:</from>

<p>Adapt or die!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>We have to learn that&#x2014;adapt or die! Those are famous words from the hon the State President. If we in the Indian community want success, if we want a good percentage poll in the forthcoming elections, I urge hon members to walk in the path of reform. Charity begins at home. If own affairs is all we have, we have to make use of it. Hon members should open their eyes. They should not shut their eyes and forget 5 000 people.</p>

<p>That is not justice, but injustice. Of the 25 million Blacks that are here they only take 5 000. Is that justice? Nevertheless it is expected of me to praise the own affairs system. Where is the own affairs system taking us? [Interjections.] I can go on and on.</p>

<p>In 1984 it was liberation and then education, but in 1989-90 it is education before liberation. We must educate the people with whom we are going to live. Mr Chairman, with these words I thank you. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#akoob">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A S AKOOB</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, it is indeed a pleasure to take part in this debate of the hon the Minister of Education and Culture and I want to touch on a few points of importance.</p>

<p>A very serious matter that is discussed every year is the appointment and placement of teachers. An example is that young unmarried female teachers are seconded to the platteland towns without any regard being given to accommodation available in these towns. Young girls and boys are just placed in far-away towns without any regard to where they are going to board. No provision is made for housing these teachers in these far-away towns. They come from all over the country, for example Natal and the Transvaal, and are never happy in the schools in which they are placed.</p>

<p>I appeal to the authorities to pay urgent attention to the needs of these teachers. Happy teachers will always bring good results in their work situation. When is the department going to provide suitable accommodation and housing for these teachers? One example is that of a young female teacher whose parents lived in Tzaneen near Pietersburg in the Northern Transvaal and who was posted to Louis Trichardt although she wanted to be in Pietersburg. Similarly teachers from Natal were posted to Pietersburg. Why could they not have posted the Natal teachers to Louis Trichardt and this teacher to Pietersburg?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_education_and_culture">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND CULTURE</person>:</from>

<p>It depends on their qualifications.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#akoob">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A S AKOOB</person>:</from>

<p>The transfer of teachers from Natal to the Transvaal for promotion is also a bone of contention. These teachers are posted to towns in the Transvaal where no housing is available. This makes for teachers that are always dissatisfied. From the first day of their promotion they are already applying for a transfer back to Natal. Consideration must be given to appointing local Transvaal teachers in promotion posts in that province.</p>

<p>There are also many dissatisfied teachers in the major cities and towns. Teachers from Lenasia teaching the same subject are placed in schools in Laudium and teachers from Laudium are placed in Lenasia. Teachers from Laudium have to travel long distances to schools in Brits, Actonville, Marlboro, Fordsburg, Azaadville, Springs and Nigel. They spend two to three hours daily travelling to and from work.</p>

<p>I want to refer to the college in Laudium and ask the hon the Minister to state his policy in this regard. What is the future of the Transvaal College of Education? There seems to be no direction. Although applications are received from Transvaal pupils, preference is given to Natal students. Why is that so? [Interjections.] I ask the hon the Minister to please spell out his future policy in regard to our only college in the Transvaal. We could use part of it as a technical <span class="col_8573-8574" refersTo="page_0312"/>college or secondary school with technical subjects.</p>

<p>Hostels are urgently needed in the Transvaal. The one in Rustenburg is a beautiful building. We need more hostels in Nelspruit, Standerton and Klerksdorp. Funds must be made available urgently.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the hon &#x201C;mbongo&#x201D; from the Eastern Transvaal will pardon me if I do not follow up his speech. What I can say of his speech is that he touched on a very real problem. Unfortunately, what I detect on that side of the House is that at least three hon members today have totally disagreed with their hon Minister. Furthermore, I detect that on that side of the House there is no cohesion or direction. It is a loose hotchpotch arrangement and nobody really knows where he belongs.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>That is in your head. You are confused.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>That hon member should go and read up Confucius&#x2014;perhaps he will get more brains. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>This is the first time that I am speaking in this House for a very long time. I want to pay tribute to the secretariat here who, whilst I was occupying the Chair&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#bandulalla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M BANDULALLA</person>:</from>

<p>[Inaudible.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>That hon member must shut up. He is being used by everybody.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#bandulalla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M BANDULALLA</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon nominated member take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>I do not take unintelligent questions from people who cannot make an intelligent contribution.</p>

<p>I want to thank the secretariat for their cooperation and assistance. Whenever I called upon them I found them available to me and found them to be very knowledgeable. I wish them well in their fields of endeavour.</p>

<p>Since this debate is on education, I want to tell hon members about a letter which the Duke of Omnium wrote to his son, Lord Silverbridge, congratulating him on his election as a Conservative Party MP in the year 1880. It is a very nice educational piece. He said the following:</p>

<block name="quote">I have known gentlemen who have felt that in becoming members of Parliament they had achieved an object for themselves instead of thinking that they had put themselves in the way of achieving something for others.</block>

</speech>

<speech by="#bandulalla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M BANDULALLA</person>:</from>

<p>That goes for you too!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Shut up! I do not need your comments! [Interjections.]</p>

<p>I continue to quote:</p>

<block name="quote">A member of Parliament should feel himself to be the servant of his country and like every other servant, he should serve. If this be distasteful to a man, he need not go into Parliament. If the harness galls him, he need not wear it. But if he takes the trappings, then he should draw the coach.</block>

<block name="quote">You are there as the guardian of your fellow-countrymen that they may be safe, that they may be prosperous, that they may be well-governed and lightly burdened, and above all, that they may be free. If you cannot feel this to be your duty, you should not be there at all.</block>

<p>This is the letter which the honourable gentleman wrote to his son. It is very appropriate in this debate because the hon the Minister of Education and Culture accepted drawing this apartheid coach. He accepted the running of this own affairs Department of Education and Culture.</p>

<p>I want to tell him that when I listened to his so-called policy speech, there was revolt within me, because I had expected a so-called policy outline. Instead, what did we get? We got a report of the <i>status quo</i> and of what that apartheid own affairs Department of Education and Culture had been doing over the past year.</p>

<p>That hon Minister was party to the acceptance of&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#bandulalla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M BANDULALLA</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon member a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member has said before that he will not take questions.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, if he has an intelligent question I will take it from him. I first want to say something about the manner in which that side of the House entrenches group areas and apartheid by supporting the Vote on <span class="col_8575-8576" refersTo="page_0313"/>Constitutional Development and Planning. When they are putting thousands of our people out on the streets, that side of the House supports it. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>That hon Minister spoke in very insulting terms about the new subject of so-called cultural studies. In his speech&#x2014;it is not a speech for reporters and I do not really know what it is&#x2014;he said the following, and I quote:</p>

<block name="quote">This subject aims at instilling an understanding and appreciation of Black, White, Coloured and Indian cultures.</block>

<p>I want to tell that hon Minister that after five years in Parliament I regret that he is so insensitive that he sits in the Joint Chamber of Parliament&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_education_and_culture">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND CULTURE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon nominated member take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>The hon nominated member will take a question!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_education_and_culture">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND CULTURE</person>:</from>

<p>Is the hon member aware that that kind of subject brings about a good relationship among race groups?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>I am going to tell the hon the Minister that this sort of language does not bring about any better understanding. In fact, if our Coloured brethren here in Parliament had to see this they would take this hon Minister to task.</p>

<p>I want to tell him that he is so insensitive that after five years in Parliament he has not listened to our so-called Coloured brethren and fellow South Africans in Parliament who resent being told that there is a so-called separate &#x201C;Coloured culture&#x201D;. There is no &#x201C;Coloured culture&#x201D; in this country. There is a culture to which the Coloured people subscribe. There is no separate &#x201C;Coloured culture&#x201D;.</p>

<p>That hon Minister should get his facts right, because what he is doing by saying these sort of things, is to create an alienation between us and our so-called fellow-South Africans who are classified by the White man&#x2019;s laws as Coloured people. Of course, that side of the House subscribes to apartheid, accepts apartheid and will make apartheid work.</p>

<p>Having said that, I want to try to drive home the fact that that hon Minister was party to accepting the so-called ten-year plan, which is aimed at bringing about equality in education. That is what the Government said. This naturally meant that large sums of money, needed for real development in so-called Indian education&#x2026; Let me just interrupt myself and say that as long as that hon Minister wants to drive the Indian education bus, he has to see to it that Indian education is brought on a par with so-called White education. However, the White people over all the years have had all the privileges in this country and have built up their educational institutions to a very high pitch.</p>

<p>In the past they had an advantage over us with regard to the total national budget for education. In the past the <i>per capita</i> expenditure on people of colour, in other words people other than White people, was far less than that on White people. The White people utilised all those opportunities in the past to build their schools and other educational institutions and to develop them to the highest levels. In short, from that position where they do not really need much more in their system, they call upon us who are still lagging behind&#x2014;there are certainly lagging standards in our communities of colour&#x2014;and they expect us to make the same type of sacrifices that they have to make.</p>

<p>What happened then? I want to charge the hon the Minister this afternoon with the fact that when those things were negotiated he hopelessly let down the community for which he is responsible. He made flowery speeches in this House calling on all of us to make sacrifices. I am the first one to say that we should make sacrifices, but the Whites should make bigger sacrifices, because their levels are far higher than those of our communities. We should not be called upon to make the same level of sacrifices as them, because they do so from a position of relative superiority. I believe there the hon the Minister let us down very badly.</p>

<p>There are a few parochial matters that I wish to discuss, but I will do that tomorrow. Today I want to speak on general issues. The hon member for Lenasia East has passed some documents on to me. I want to make it very clear that I have the highest degree of respect for the administrative staff and for all the educators in this country. We may have some bad eggs somewhere along the line, but do we not have them in all avenues. It is a pity that a very senior official is affected by this document. I firstly find it very strange that a <span class="col_8577-8578" refersTo="page_0314"/>document marked &#x201C;secret&#x201D; ended up in the lap of the hon member for Lenasia East. He has not told me how he received it.</p>

<p>This is damning because I believe it would appear that there surely was some leakage in that Ministers&#x2019; Council, or that the people who are the responsible custodians of these documents are not such good custodians after all. I want to say that I unfortunately find it difficult to agree to entrust the future of our people to such incompetent hands that they cannot even look after such important documents. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>What does this document say? I merely want to repeat the last paragraph&#x2014;the finding&#x2014;which the hon member for Lenasia East could not complete. He asked me to do so on his behalf.</p>

<p>Paragraph 10.1 of this secret document which was drawn up by the Chief Director of the Budget and of Auxiliary Services says:</p>

<block name="quote">Having regard to what I have said in paragraph 8.2.3 above and the findings of the External Moderator and Moderator of the Joint Matriculation Board for Biology, Prof N Grobbelaar, I have come to the conclusion that Mr G Khadaroo, the person who carried out the function of remarking the biology script, favoured Miss Singh and was aware of the fact that candidate No 068128 and Miss D A Singh, the daughter of the Executive Director of the Department of Education and Culture, were one and the same person when he was dealing with the Biology scripts. 1 was however unable to establish who conveyed this information to Mr Khadaroo&#x2014;it could have been either Dr Morgan Naidoo or Mr A K Singh. I doubt, however, whether it was Mr Naidoo for he could have advised Mr Khadaroo that the candidate required only five more marks to obtain <i>a</i> Standard Grade Pass in biology.</block>

<p>*Mr Chairman, when taking the answers which the hon the Minister gave the hon member for Lenasia Central in this House on 14 March into account, I want to say that the implications of his answer and of what appears in this report, lead me to one conclusion only.</p>

<p>&#x2020;That is that the hon the Minister of Education and Culture is honour-bound to resign. [Interjections.] The hon member for Southern Natal can laugh as much as he likes. Of course he can laugh as much as he likes. I think he should look at himself in the mirror right now. They are the people who claim that they want clean administration. [Interjections.] Where does clean administration come from now, when they are trying to cover up for that hon Minister who deliberately misled Parliament by saying the following when he responded to the hon member for Lenasia Central?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member Mr Abram must withdraw the word &#x201C;deliberately&#x201D;.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Fine, Sir. He did not deliberately mislead us, but he misled us. Will that satisfy Mr Chairman?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member may proceed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>This is what the hon the Minister told this House. He said that he had to disclose the truth lest by omission an unpardonable injustice is committed not only against the Chief Executive Director and his daughter, but also against the image of his department. What more damning evidence does one need than the publication of this secret report and the availability of this secret report to tarnish the image of that hon Minister&#x2019;s department and more so of that hon Minister himself?</p>

<p>The hon the Minister said further:</p>

<block name="quote">Both the internal and the external moderators&#x2014;after independently re-examining the relevant examination script of candidate No 068128&#x2014;arrived at the favourable decision that the pupil in question had in fact passed the examination. Moderators do not know the names of candidates.</block>

<p>Something is said here about the particular candidate having been five marks short of passing biology on the standard grade.</p>

<p>I think if that side of the House is serious about so-called clean administration, they must not come and talk here of wounded animals or whatever. I am talking to hon members this afternoon about the facts that are here on the table before us. The facts show beyond reasonable doubt that there has been some degree of impropriety and that the hon the Minister of Education and Culture misled this House in the reply he gave us on 14 March 1989.</p>

<p>*In Parliament one may perhaps fool around with women. One could perhaps steal someone <span class="col_8579-8580" refersTo="page_0315"/>else&#x2019;s money. Apparently such things are acceptable. There is only one thing which one may or dare not do in Parliament. That is lying to Parliament. That is what the hon the Minister did. I think that the hon the Minister very definitely&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member may not say that the hon the Minister lied. I ask the hon member to withdraw that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I want to say that the hon the Minister misled the House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I am asking the hon member to withdraw that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I withdraw the word &#x201C;lied&#x201D;. I am saying that he misled us.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member must withdraw the full sentence, not only the word &#x201C;lied&#x201D;, in the context in which it was said.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I shall put it differently. I want to say that the hon the Minister&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point of order: Since the hon member is directing criticism at a person who does not understand Afrikaans, is it courteous of him to use that language?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>That is not a point of order.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I am certain that the hon member for Reservoir Hills will agree with me that if I am speaking to my hon Minister of Education and Culture&#x2014;Francois as you call him, Sir&#x2014;I expect him to understand the other official language of this country. [Interjections.] I want to tell hon members that I have not seen the inside of a university. That hon Minister must have some university degrees behind his name; otherwise he would not have been a principal of a school.</p>

<p>*Mr Chairman, I want to say that the hon the Minister misled us and because he misled us, we demand his resignation.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_education_and_culture">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND CULTURE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon member take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I will take any question.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_education_and_culture">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND CULTURE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, is the hon member giving us this venom this afternoon because his agent failed to convince me to back him as Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, when somebody is driven into a corner, the only thing they can do is to try and look for faults in me. I want to tell that hon Minister that at no time did I have such aspirations and at no time did I send any agents to him. The agent that he talks of is a figment of his imagination. The hon Minister has no defence whatsoever. He must not now stoop to the lowest of low levels. I have regarded him as an honourable person all these years and I hope that he will not be as obstinate as others here who knew that the time had come to resign and go and were not prepared to do so. When the time came for me to go, I was honourable enough to go. I did not come and beg hon members and say that I would come to them with two or three other hon members and that they must accept me back and give me the position of Chairman. I did not do that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#bandulalla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M BANDULALLA</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon member take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I am not prepared to answer questions from that jack-in-the-box. I am not interested in him. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Hon members will not interject when the hon member is speaking.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I speak to intelligent people and I consider the hon the Minister of Education and Culture extremely intelligent. [Time expired.]</p>

<p>Debate interrupted.</p>

</speech>

<adjournment>

<p>The House adjourned at <recordedTime time="1989-05-11T18:24:00">18h24.</recordedTime></p>

</adjournment>

</debateSection>

<p><b>COMMITTEE REPORTS:</b></p>

<p><i>General Affairs:</i></p>

<p>1. Report of the Joint Committee on Environment Affairs on the <i>Environment</i> <i>Conservation Bill</i> [B 60&#x2014;89 (GA)], dated 10 May 1989, as follows:</p>

<p>The Joint Committee on Environment Affairs, having considered the subject of the <i>Environment Conservation Bill</i> [B 60&#x2014;89 (G&#x00C1;)], referred to it, begs to report the Bill with amendments [B 60A&#x2014;89 (GA)].</p>

<p>2. Report of the Joint Committee on Environment Affairs on the <i>Engineering Profession of South Africa Bill</i> [B 74&#x2014;89 (GA)], dated 10 May 1989, as follows:</p>

<p>3. Report of the Joint Committee on Manpower and Mineral and Energy Affairs on the <i>Energy Amendment Bill</i> [B 88&#x2014;89 (GA)], dated 10 May 1989, as follows:</p>

<p>The Joint Committee on Manpower and Mineral and Energy Affairs, having considered the subject of the <i>Energy Amendment Bill</i> [B 88&#x2014;89 (GA)], referred to it, begs to report the Bill with amendments [B 88A&#x2014;89 (GA)].</p>

<p>The House Committee on Cultural Affairs Bill (House of Assembly) and Cultural Institutions Bill (House of Assembly), having considered the subject of the <i>Cultural Affairs Bill (House of Assembly)</i> [B 75&#x2014;89 (HA)], referred to it, begs to report the Bill with amendments [B 75A&#x2014;89 (HA)].</p>
